David Schwimmer Reunites With His Famous Red Sweater From ‘Friends’ in Nostalgic Quince Campaign

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Variety may receive an affiliate commission.

David Schwimmer is back on the hunt for a red sweater — 25 years after a nearly identical garment revealed one of the biggest plot twists on “Friends.”

The actor stars in a new holiday campaign for Quince, titled “The Red Sweater,” which sees him chasing down the brand’s signature cashmere sweater across New York City, only to discover that seemingly everyone already owns one. The 60-second short marks the retailer’s first major celebrity-led campaign. Related Stories NFL to Face New Trial in Sunday Ticket Class-Action Lawsuit to Determine Financial Damages

The premise is a wink to the beloved Season 8 “Friends” episode “The One With the Red Sweater,” in which Ross Geller (Schwimmer) unwittingly reveals himself as the father of Rachel Green’s baby after claiming ownership of a mysterious red sweater left in her apartment. The episode, which aired in October 2001, was also directed by Schwimmer.

“When Quince came to me with this idea, I thought it was really funny — after all, a red sweater and I go back a long way,” Schwimmer said in a statement. “This time, I’m chasing the sweater, and somehow everyone else has one. Even the dog.”

According to the company, Schwimmer was closely involved in developing the campaign, from its script through the final edit.

The new campaign introduces Coulis Red, a limited new colorway available across a selection of its 100% Mongolian cashmere styles. The collection includes a women’s oversized crewneck sweater for $129.90 and a men’s crewneck for $69.90, both available beginning Nov. 9 on Quince’s website.

“The Red Sweater” premieres Nov. 9 on YouTube and Instagram, with additional edits rolling out across social media. The campaign will run across the U.S., Canada and the U.K., marking Quince’s first international, multichannel brand activation.

The Blue Wave Could Mean a Rising Tide for Songwriters — Here’s Why (Guest Post)

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

David Israelite is the president & CEO of the National Music Publishers’ Association, the trade association representing American music publishers and their songwriting partners. Variety welcomes responsible commentary, please send ideas to music@variety.com.

The political tidal wave that swept across America will have consequences far beyond Washington. For the music industry, and particularly for the songwriters and music publishers who create the songs that power it, the election is the beginning of a new era. Related Stories NFL to Face New Trial in Sunday Ticket Class-Action Lawsuit to Determine Financial Damages

Democrats have captured control of both the House and Senate. A new, younger generation of lawmakers is coming to Washington. This generation rode a growing sentiment that is skeptical of Big Tech – from AI to oligarchs who have had a stronghold on Washington.

The Judiciary committees will have new leadership. And the technology companies that have increasingly dominated our industry will face a very different political landscape.

For songwriters, these changes present significant opportunities.

To be clear, songwriter issues do not favor any political party. Songwriters have a right to be paid fairly for their work. That has always been a bipartisan cause.

Our strongest champions have been both Republicans and Democrats. Remember, the Music Modernization Act was introduced by Congressmen Hakeem Jeffries (D-NY) – the potential next Speaker of the House – and Doug Collins (R-GA). And that is precisely why this election creates such an interesting opportunity.

No Congressional committees matter more to the music industry than the House and Senate Judiciary Committees and their intellectual property subcommittees.

These lawmakers oversee copyright law, consider legislation affecting music licensing, and help determine how our intellectual property system adapts to new technology.

With Democrats taking control of Congress, these committees will have new chairs, new agendas and new opportunities to examine issues that have too often been overlooked.

That does not mean our Republican champions will become unimportant. We have spent years building relationships with members of both parties who understand the value of American intellectual property. Those relationships remain essential.

But new leadership creates opportunities for new conversations, perspectives, and hopefully, new legislation.

No issue presents a greater long-term challenge to the creative community than artificial intelligence. And AI faces a new political reality.

The question is no longer whether AI will transform the music business. It already has. The question is whether the companies developing these technologies will be allowed to build their businesses on the work of human creators without permission or compensation.

For too long, the technology industry has promoted a dangerous proposition: that innovation requires weakening copyright protections.

The incoming Congress has an opportunity to establish clear rules on the use of copyrighted works for AI training, demand meaningful transparency from technology companies, and protect the voices and identities of human creators.

These should not be partisan questions. The principle that someone cannot simply take another person’s property to build a commercial business transcends political ideology.

It’s time to reorient the relationship between Big Tech and songwriters.

Perhaps the greatest opportunity presented by the new Congress is a fresh examination of how the federal government treats the individuals who create music compared with the giant tech companies that exploit it.

For decades, songwriters have operated under an extraordinary system of government regulation.

The mechanical compulsory license regulating songwriters dates back to 1909. It’s outdated and protects the wrong parties.

The consent decrees governing ASCAP and BMI date back to 1941. Think about that. Rules established when Americans listened to music on radios from their local broadcasting station still constrain how songwriters negotiate compensation in the age of streaming, AI, and trillion-dollar technology companies.

Meanwhile, the largest technology companies have accumulated unprecedented economic power.

We have somehow created a system in which the government regulates the songwriters while the technology giants that depend on their work wield enormous marketplace power.

That imbalance deserves serious congressional scrutiny.

The new Congress should examine whether antiquated compulsory licenses and consent decrees continue to serve their intended purposes or instead provide powerful digital platforms with negotiating advantages at the expense of creators.

This is not an argument against antitrust enforcement. Quite the opposite. It is an argument for applying competition principles fairly, consistently and in ways that reflect the marketplace as it actually exists today.

A system designed to protect competition should not become a tool that allows the largest corporations in the world to squeeze the smallest creators.

An exciting consequence of a wave election is the arrival of so many new members of Congress.

The music industry has an extraordinary story to tell. American songs are among our most valuable cultural exports. Songwriters are entrepreneurs and small-business owners. And the economic benefits of music extend to communities in every state and congressional district.

Our job is to make sure these new lawmakers understand that protecting songwriters means protecting American jobs, American innovation and American intellectual property.

For too long, the rules governing songwriters have reflected the marketplace of yesterday rather than the realities of tomorrow.

The election belonged to the Democrats. The opportunity belongs to songwriters.

Former Crystal Palace defender and current Australia national team head coach Tony Popovic has emerged as a left-field contender to replace Pierre Sage, with the French manager on the verge of being sacked at Selhurst Park.

According to talkSPORT, Parish has decided to end Pierre Sage’s tenure after just 16 matches in charge, with the Eagles sitting bottom of the league table.

Palace have endured a miserable opening to the 2026/27 campaign, suffering seven defeats in their first ten Premier League fixtures.

Sunday’s 3-1 home defeat against Liverpool further increased the pressure on Sage, leaving the club facing an increasingly worrying relegation battle.

Former Tottenham manager Thomas Frank has emerged as the early frontrunner to replace the Frenchman, but Palace are also reportedly considering several alternatives, including ex-defender Popovic.

According to the report, Popovic is among the names being considered by Palace as they assess potential replacements for Sage.

The 53-year-old currently manages the Australian national team and has extensive coaching experience across domestic and international football.

The former central defender joined the club on a free transfer from Sanfrecce Hiroshima in August 2001 and went on to make over 120 appearances across five seasons.

Popovic eventually earned the captain’s armband, playing a key role in Palace’s 2003/04 promotion campaign to the Premier League.

After retiring, Popovic returned to Selhurst Park in 2011 to serve as an assistant manager under Dougie Freedman.

Since then, he has had a successful head coaching career across the A-League, Asia, and international management with Australia. He has built a reputation for organising competitive teams.

Popovic’s long-standing relationship with Crystal Palace makes him an interesting candidate to steer the club out of trouble.

Costco’s $1.50 hot dogs being resold for $18 on black market delivery service

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Costco’s iconic food court items are being sold on new delivery apps for a massive markup in price, with the iconic $1.50 hot dog and drink costing $18. Seriously.

For a lot of Costco customers, a trip to the warehouse isn’t complete without picking up something from the food court. Some shoppers grab a hot dog and drink before they do any shopping, while others wait to grab a treat before they exit.

The iconic hot dog and drink combo still costs $1.50 in the US, with Costco CEO Ron Vachris promising to never raise it.

However, if you’re in Southern California and can’t just dash to your nearest warehouse, there is a new food delivery startup that you can order from. Just don’t expect the cheap food items to come, well, cheap.

As per SFGate, a new delivery service called NoTip is actually delivering Costco’s food court items to customers. Costco is available on Uber Eats, but you aren’t able to order any of the hot food or sweet treats.

That’s where NoTip comes in, but they do charge a big markup. Speaking to SFGate, NoTip’s Jack Chen noted that the $1.50 hot dog and drink combo actually costs $18 on the app.

“A lot of offices are ordering from me. It’s the company’s money. As long as you’re not too expensive, I mean, they’ve got the money to blow,” Chen told SFGate. “You’d be surprised how some people are just craving it specifically.”

Costco releases brand-new food court dessert and it’s the “best” yet

Costco’s most popular items actually aren’t available on Uber Eats or DoorDash

Costco CEO promises he’ll never raise price of $1.50 hot dog after joining taste test trend

The NoTip founder argues that “it only adds more money” to Costco, seeing as his service is pretty busy. “I love Costco, and I would like to get more business to Costco.”

It isn’t the first time that customers have ended up paying a premium for items that aren’t on delivery services.

Some drivers have been charging $50 for burger runs to In-N-Out because the chain has rejected services like Uber Eats and DoorDash.

Congratulations to These 2026 Appalachian Trail Finishers (Part 9)

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Every year, thousands of hopeful thru-hikers set off on a journey of nearly 2,200 miles. The Appalachian Trail stretches from Springer Mountain in Georgia to Mount Katahdin in Maine.  Hikers encounter unforgettable scenery ranging from sprawling mountain vistas to dense forests. The trail is as challenging as it is beautiful,  pushing hikers to their limits both physically and mentally.

Of all the ambitious hikers who embark on this trek, less than half will successfully complete a thru-hike. Finishing the AT is a life-changing accomplishment that requires physical determination, mental toughness, and a little bit of luck. Hikers from all walks of life are impacted by their time spent on the trail, and we are thrilled to spotlight the incredible hikers who completed the AT in 2026!

Have you just finished your AT thru-hike? If so, please celebrate your finish with us by submitting a photo through the form below so we can feature you in a subsequent post. We can’t wait to share your incredible achievement!

“Completed my triple crown on Katahdin – what a wonderful and frustratingly difficult-to-articulate journey it’s been! Feeling incredibly fortunate for all the experiences and community that thru-hiking has brought me, and I can’t wait to experience more!” -Meemaw

“Congratulations to all the 2026 Thru-Hikers. A big thanks to my family and friends. I would not have been able to do it without you.” -Old School

“God Bless America!! Semper Fi!!” -Old Man River & G-force

Congratulations to all the 2026 AT finishers — your achievements are remarkable and worth celebrating!

Did you hike the AT this year? Take the 2026 Appalachian Trail Thru-Hiker Survey and help out next year’s hikers.

After completing the AT, the pair moved to east Tennessee to work as backpacking guides in Great Smoky Mountains National Park. Struggling £26m West Ham man eyed for escape transfer to South American giants

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

West Ham signed Taty Castellanos for a deal worth around £26m in January as they bid to salvage their Premier League status. WHUFC came up short despite their new striker netting six goals in 18 Premier League games, but his form has not continued in the Championship.

Now, a report from Argentine news outlet El Intransigente says Castellanos could find an escape route back to his home country. River Plate are keen on signing a new striker and the Hammers man sits atop their shortlist of targets.

Despite being from Argentina, Castellanos has never played senior football in his native. He has played in Chile, Uruguay, Spain and the United States, as well as in England with West Ham.

West Ham have not had an easy time in the Championship, dropping to 9th after Saturday’s loss to Blackburn Rovers. However, with 37 goals scored in 15 matches, they are still the league’s highest-scoring team by far.

Despite this, and his pedigree at a higher level, Castellanos has not found form in front of goal. He started the competitive campaign with a Carabao Cup brace against Portsmouth and scored in the same competition against Southampton in August too.

But, the 28-year-old has found the back of the net just once in the Championship, that being in the 6-0 home thumping of Wrexham. Castellanos has chipped in with a strong tally of five assists, but a total of 3.46 xG shows how he is underperforming in terms of goalscoring (FotMob).

It has seen him drop from the WHUFC starting lineup for four of the last six league games. Now, it brings questions over his future as River Plate lurk.

As highlighted, West Ham are scoring plenty of goals. However, they’re not coming from the strikers.

Of the natural no. 9’s on the books, Joel Piroe is the top scorer on four. Pablo earns praise for his physicality and work rate but two goals in 28 career games for WHUFC shows he leaves a lot to be desired in front of goal.

By selling Castellanos, the Hammers can raise funds and free up space in the squad for a new talisman to join. They can ease the burden on Piroe while Pablo can mainly be used in the impact substitute role he tends to be better in.

It could breathe new life into Scott Parker’s attack with clear threats out wide and in midfield like Jarrod Bowen, Edvin Austbo, Manor Solomon, Divine Mukasa and Mohamadou Kante.

Although World of Warcraft: Forever has brought back a record number of lapsed players and boasts the biggest concurrent player launch in the MMO's history, fans aren't entirely pleased with it – or, at least, not with Blizzard's treatment of dungeons.

The point of contention here isn't Forever's inclusion of dungeons or the way that any of the nine new group activities play out – it's to do with the heavy nerfs that Blizzard applied to experience points players can gain from killing enemies inside the dungeons. These rolled in over WoW: Forever's Beta period, when developers cut XP earned in dungeons (resulting in a massive gap between them and Classic's own dungeons) to encourage questing.

Those cuts were not popular, to say the least, spurring discussion among fans questioning why there was such a difference between WoW: Forever and Classic in terms of dungeon XP – and, evidently, Blizzard's been listening. Just yesterday, the studio confirmed as much, writing on its forums, "We deployed a hotfix that increased kill experience in dungeons by 50%, so it feels better to go back in for more runs."

The thing is, however… that's still not enough for lots of folks. A thread on the matter over on Reddit proves so, with comments expressing discontent despite Blizzard's hotfix. It still leaves a lot of room when considering Classic's dungeon XP. As one person asks, "So 37.5% instead of 25% of Classic?" Another adds, "Great that they are listening. But it seems like that would still be a big nerf compared to normal exp?"

A separate post pokes fun at the situation with a meme, outlining how dungeon XP is "still only 1/3 of Classic" – oof. Replies are similar to those aforementioned, with one exclaiming that "Blizzard's shamelessly bad at math or they're trolling." Unfortunately, I'm not sure it's either myself. I do think the aim was to balance dungeon XP with questing, but it's clear this might not have been the best way to go about striking this hoped-for balance.

I wouldn't be too surprised if we saw more changes roll out to World of Warcraft: Forever with how the community is reacting to Blizzard's first remedy to its brutal dungeon XP nerfs – but I suppose only time will tell for certain.

World of Warcraft: Forever devs say "this launch was crazier than the insanity we imagined" as MMO fans dub it "the smoothest WoW launch" ever.

Équipe de France : Zidane appelle un joueur de deuxième division !

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

C'est la surprise majeure de ce rassemblement de novembre. Pour pallier le forfait sur blessure de Pierre Kalulu, le sélectionneur Zinédine Zidane a décidé d'appeler pour la première fois Saël Kumbedi en équipe de France ce lundi 9 novembre 2026. Une trajectoire fulgurante pour le latéral droit évoluant en deuxième division allemande.

Le premier jour du rassemblement à Clairefontaine a réservé un rebondissement de taille dans les rangs tricolores. En effet, Pierre Kalulu a dû déclarer forfait en raison d'une lésion aux ischios du membre inférieur gauche contractée ce week-end avec la Juventus. Face à ce coup dur dans le couloir droit, Zinédine Zidane s'est tourné vers Saël Kumbedi, alors que le joueur de 21 ans avait initialement été retenu par Gérald Baticle avec les Espoirs pour disputer deux rencontres amicales face à l'Italie à Brest et contre l'Angleterre à Bristol.

Comptant déjà neuf sélections avec les Bleuets, le défenseur franchit donc un cap décisif. Pour régler la pénurie à ce poste, le staff technique a délibérément privilégié la jeunesse du joueur de Wolfsburg plutôt que l'expérience de Jonathan Clauss, pourtant performant à Nice du haut de ses 34 ans et de ses quatorze sélections (2 buts). Un choix fort.

Avant d'atteindre le groupe de Zinédine Zidane, Saël Kumbedi a connu un parcours jalonné d'étapes intenses. Formé au Havre, le natif de Stains avait rejoint l'Olympique Lyonnais en 2022 contre un chèque d'un million d'euros, découvrant la Ligue 1 à seulement 17 ans sous la houlette de Peter Bosz avant d'enchaîner sous les ordres de Laurent Blanc. Au total, le défenseur a disputé 62 matches sous le maillot des Gones, dont quatre apparitions sur la scène européenne en Ligue Europa lors de l'exercice 2024-2025.

Prêté au VfL Wolfsburg à l'été 2025, le latéral a ensuite été transféré définitivement le 1er janvier 2026 pour un montant de six millions d'euros. Toutefois, l'institution allemande, championne de Bundesliga en 2009 et installée dans l'élite sans discontinuer depuis 1997, a été reléguée en mai 2026 lors d'un barrage contre Paderborn (0-0, 2-1 a.p.). Un coup dur pour la formation allemande. Resté fidèle au club en 2. Bundesliga, Kumbedi est rapidement devenu un titulaire indiscutable sous les ordres de Tobias Strobl, malgré une blessure musculaire lui ayant fait manquer trois matches.

Cette convocation inattendue concrétise la passerelle directe établie entre la sélection A et les moins de 21 ans tricolores. Présent chez les Espoirs, le défenseur Nathan Zézé avait d'ailleurs rapporté le discours tenu par Zinédine Zidane :

Le parcours de Saël Kumbedi en équipe de France démarre ainsi dans ce contexte de renouvellement. Les Bleus disputeront deux matches de Ligue des nations capitaux au cours de cette trêve internationale. Ils commenceront par un déplacement face à l'Italie à Milan, programmé le jeudi 12 novembre 2026 à 20h45. L'équipe de France recevra ensuite la Turquie à Bordeaux, le dimanche 15 novembre 2026 à 20h45.

The pillars of AI are shaking beneath our feet

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strap yourselves in, whatever happens next isn't going to be pretty.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I've spent far too much of my life covering the rampant ascent of AI. A juggernaut of tech development, destined to change the world into some sort of sci-fi utopia (if you believe its advocates) or fated to come crashing down around our ears, taking a large chunk of the world economy with it. Or the end of the world as we know it, according to some.

And now, the pillars upon which the AI revolution sits seem to be shaking beneath it. The most recent tremors began with the wobbling of US AI-led tech stocks in mid-October, after a similar descent in China earlier this year. Then at the end of the month, while many tech companies reported huge amounts of revenue in their quarterly financial results, some AI tech share prices still slid under a black cloud of investor doubt.

None more so than Alphabet, the owner of Google. A $500 billion wipe in stock price, led by the admission that the company's own AI search features were eating into its ad revenue, rocked the market. A wider tech sell-off, fuelled by fears regarding the eventual profitability of AI and a potential upcoming Democrat victory in the US midterms, rattled the valuations of many AI tech companies—alongside the chip manufacturers sitting underneath them.

All this, of course, amid a backdrop of increasing AI-related concerns across multiple fields. Rogue AI agents. Increasing AI-powered cybersecurity incidents. Predictions of the end of civilisation, brought on by rampant AI development without proper oversight. Fears of job market ruination. AI psychosis. The list goes on.

Then, the so-called blue wave came to pass. Democrats won control of both the US House and the Senate. Such sweeping political changes can rarely be ascribed to a single factor, but US voter polling indicates that data center expansion was a key source of voter frustration. The current Republican administration has gone all in on AI, and much of the US public seems displeased at the idea of massive AI tech facilities springing up left, right, and center across the country.

Jensen Huang has hit the headlines over allegations surrounding his involvement with an alleged Chinese spy.

Trillions were wiped off US tech stock values as investors sold off increasing numbers of shares in tech companies, amid further fears that the great AI infrastructure buildout was about to be reigned in by political changes. Long-called-for regulation of AI and AI companies seemed—and indeed, still seems—ever more likely.

And now, Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has hit the headlines over allegations surrounding his involvement with an alleged Chinese spy. At first, it was reported that Alice Huang (no relation) had a "close relationship" with the Nvidia chief.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

Now, Jensen Huang has himself admitted that the two were engaged in an affair, and Nvidia has launched an internal investigation into concerns related to national security. The timing could not be worse for the US tech market. At the time of writing, Nvidia's share value has sunk in pre-market trading.

The company is, in many ways, the backbone of the AI revolution, the chief provider of the chips behind the sweeping AI rollout that has changed the tech landscape over the past few years, and the creator of the CUDA platform that underpins it. Huang is its charismatic leader, the relentless optimist behind this AI-led future we're supposedly about to enjoy. A scandal at this stage is likely the last thing any tech investor wants to see.

So, what now? While the long-mooted AI bubble burst has yet to materialise in full, it's certainly looking like the relentless "all the money in the world" optimism phase is coming to an end. Investors are shaken, with worries about long-term profitability continuing to eat into the market, alongside US national security concerns now dominating the headlines.

The train with no brakes is rocking wildly against the tracks—and unfortunately, given AI and big tech's role in the global economy, we're all stuck in the carriages behind it.

The relentless "all the money in the world" optimism phase is coming to an end.

I'm no economist. I can't tell you if this is the beginning of the AI bubble pop, or whether this is merely an extremely turbulent time in the AI success story. Certainly, it seems like the horse has already bolted, and that AI is here to stay in some form or fashion. Those hoping for a return to the good old, pre-AI days are almost certain to be disappointed.

But, having reported on so many "AI doomsayers" over the past few years, I can tell you that a fall from grace has been predicted by many. Such a massive, world-changing technological development, fuelled by seemingly impossible amounts of investment, has always seemed destined to face some sort of financial reckoning.

That reckoning, I believe, has never looked closer. May you live in interesting times, as the old saying goes. And what happens next? Well, it's going to be interesting, at the very least.

4. Best mini PC: Minisforum AtomMan G7 PT

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Soybean futures gave up earlier gains on Monday as traders looked ahead to a USDA crop report due this week

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent and U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer pressed China to carry out its past commitments, including agricultural purchases, during a videoconference with Vice Premier He Lifeng. Soybean futures jumped by the most in more than two weeks following the U.S. appeal, according to Bloomberg, before giving up those gains.

Bessent raised concerns about recent Chinese actions and said Beijing must follow through on the pledges it made at the Busan meeting, the Treasury Department said. He said further advances hinge on tangible outcomes and that Washington will evaluate how China carries out the deal.

Greer said China must deliver tangible steps on buying farm goods, on rare earths and on other critical minerals, according to the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative. The office said the administration will monitor compliance with past commitments while pursuing balanced trade with China.

Traders balanced fresh optimism about Chinese buying against forecasts that the U.S. Department of Agriculture will show soybean output edging nearer to an all-time high, according to Bloomberg. Futures have fallen for four straight weeks.

China voiced "serious concerns" over the latest U.S. restrictions on trade and the economy during the call, according to Bloomberg. The two sides engaged in "frank, in-depth, and constructive exchanges" and agreed to deepen cooperation while keeping disagreements in check, Bloomberg said, citing a Chinese state news agency account of the call.

Chinese state-owned companies had reserved at least 20 shiploads of U.S. soybeans in mid-October, roughly 1.3 million tons, their first purchases since the late-September summit between President Donald Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping. Beijing has committed to import at least 25 million tons of U.S. soybeans a year through 2028, along with $17 billion a year in other U.S. farm goods.

In September, Bessent said some Chinese deliverables had fallen short and that China was running late on some of its agricultural commitments, according to Bloomberg. The two governments agreed to push their trade truce out to Jan. 10.

Thomas Tuchel clarifies Jordan Pickford's England role amid No 1 uncertainty

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Tuchel had suggested Pickford may split game time with understudy James Trafford

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Thomas Tuchel has confirmed Jordan Pickford remains England’s number one but suggested he may again split game time with understudy James Trafford this week.

The Everton goalkeeper has been one of the first names on the team sheet ever since Sir Gareth Southgate made him first choice heading into the 2018 World Cup in Russia.

Pickford has played a key role in every tournament since but found himself in the unusual position of watching from bench at the start of England’s first meet-up since the World Cup.

The 93-cap keeper only played in half of the four-game autumn camp as Tuchel decided to start up-and-coming Leeds player Trafford in the first two fixtures.

Asked if Pickford was no longer number one, Tuchel said: “No, he is. But nothing has changed. The competition is on there as well, like for the outfield players.

“Traff gets regular starts now, that’s what we wanted. James Trafford did well. They shared the matches last time.

“I’m not sure what we will do. There’s an opportunity that we maybe do this again.

“I have to speak with our goalkeeper coach. But the goalkeepers, they are a bit of a unit for themselves, and rightly so.”

Tuchel has two more Nations League matches to round off a hectic year, with Thursday’s Wembley clash against Croatia followed by a trip to World Cup winners Spain on Sunday.

“It’s slightly different (to outfield players), but when do I have a chance to see another goalkeeper, if not in Nations League? If not in high-pressure matches?” the head coach asked.

“So, we had James against Spain, we asked ourselves, ‘Is this fair for James Trafford to straightaway go against Spain?’.

“But I think it’s exactly what we need because what happens if Jordan Pickford is injured? What happens if he’s not available?

“So, it’s better to test it. Jordan knows about this. That we want to use Nations League for exactly that.

“But at the moment, nothing has changed. At the moment, he’s the number one and he just feels a bit of competition, which is good for him.”

Not only is it pushing Pickford but, Tuchel says, has shown the 32-year-old’s quality of character.

“I was very impressed and a bit surprised by his reaction when we spoke to him,” the England boss added.

“I thought he’d be less of a fan than he proved to be because he said he totally understands it.

“He impressed me a lot last time. We spoke to him, it was a very short conversation. It was very positive.

“He said, ‘Yeah, let’s do this, Traff deserves it and I will push him’. And he didn’t change at all.

“If he changed, he changed even to the positive, he pushed Traff, he gave him a good feeling. So very impressive, he did it very well.”

It seems like the board game community is really keen on Arkham Horror Remastered, because the crowdfunding page has been kept going for an extra 108 hours past the original deadline thanks to their support.

It's only been a couple of months since this revamp of the beloved, long-out-of-print horror game was announced, but the project has gone from strength to strength. Arkham Horror Remastered quickly hit its crowdfunding goal after its Gamefound campaign went live, and now – thanks to the 'Endgame' extension that's been running for over four days – it's sitting on more than $5.7 million. In fact, $803K has been added since this extended time period began.

In essence, Endgame creates a 10-minute timer. Every new backer resets that timer, and it'll only end when no-one else has backed the project.

As the name would suggest, this Remastered version harkens back to an older version of Arkham Horror – the 2005 edition, to be precise. While the current version is well-liked and still widely available at Amazon, it's very different; it's quicker and more streamlined than the crunchy, in-depth '05 board game.

So, why are fans trying to keep the campaign going? For starters, Arkham Horror Remastered will only be available via this Gamefound campaign – it isn't coming to retail at any point, so this is your only chance of adding it to your collection of the best board games. In addition, a new gameplay element is added with every 24-hour milestone. An exclusive art postcard is also added for every six hours the timer is kept running.

Stretch goals that have already been unlocked range from a host of new characters and monsters to physical elements like token holders modelled after eldritch gates. An Arkham Asylum postcard is still to go, being promised alongside another hero, Louis Brown.

There's no telling when this train will screech to a halt – so if you want the board game and haven't pledged yet, I'd do so now while you still have a chance. You won't get another opportunity, after all.

TL;DR: Amazon Prime members can get $20 off each of their first two qualifying Uber Eats restaurant orders through Amazon until Nov. 18 at 11:59 p.m. PST. Each order must total at least $25 before taxes and fees and after any other qualifying discounts.

This may just be the frugal part of me talking, but I’ve always thought Uber Eats was one of the more expensive food delivery apps (right up there with Postmates). So when I heard Amazon Prime members could now get $0 delivery fees through Uber Eats, I was pretty excited. I still have questions about some of those clearly inflated restaurant prices, but free delivery is a step in the right direction.

Right now, eligible Prime members can get $20 off each of their first two Uber Eats restaurant orders through Amazon, for up to $40 off across two orders. Each order needs to total at least $25 before taxes and fees, after any other qualifying discounts, and the offer expires Nov. 18 at 11:59 p.m. PST. Go to Amazon’s Uber Eats page while signed in to your Prime account and tap “Allow” to activate the $0 delivery-fee benefit. From there, browse participating restaurants and check that your qualifying $20 discount appears before submitting your order. You don’t need a separate Uber Eats account, but you do need to order through Amazon’s website or shopping app — not the Uber Eats app or website.

The benefit is available to U.S. Prime members, including those with Prime for Young Adults and Prime Access memberships. Restaurant selection depends on your location, so check what’s available near you.

I wouldn’t pay for a year just to get two discounted meals, but the trial is worth considering if you also plan to use Prime for Black Friday shopping.

I’ve Played Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Way Too Much To Enjoy The Remake As Much As It Deserves

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Game DiaryRemakeSwitch 2The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time

I’m not sure exactly how many times I’ve played The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, but it’s a lot. Back in the days of the Nintendo 64, we weren’t drowning in new releases or free-to-play forever games. You played what you had. And if what you had was good, you played it again and again.

Ocarina of Time was very good and I probably played it a couple of times a year. The remake is gorgeous. It’s also more faithful than I was expecting. I’ve gone through these motions before. Never like this, but enough to not feel as impressed as I should be.

I’m currently making my way through the Gerudo Fortress. My kids and I took turns throughout most of the weekend (I may have had a habit of passing the controller every time we had to go back to the Temple of Time, transform back into Young Link, and run back across Hyrule Field to unlock the next step in a quest). I’ve shown them the original game, but they still feel like this is something completely different. I wish I could feel the same.

I don’t remember every secret in the game and I was never much of a completionist. I would be useless trying to unlock the Biggoron Sword without a guide. But all of the beats along the critical path remain surprisingly familiar, pushed to the forefront of my mind as I encounter each one in the remake. The many ways in which the new version has been streamlined have only served to further that feeling of going back through the motions. The tightly orchestrated logic of the puzzles also reinforces that.

These motions should not be underestimated. The controls are responsive and satisfying. The landscapes are beautiful. And the fundamental gameplay loops remain beyond reproach. Ocarina of Time serves as the foundational blueprint for 3D dungeon puzzles in Zelda. Nearly 30 years later, they still feel remarkably clever, but my fondness for them is closer to the kind of nostalgic recognition I might feel while looking back through the handwritten notes in an old yearbook. You kinda had to be thereÂ back in 1998 to get the full effect.

Being there in 2026 with the remake, I’m surprised just how much of the original has been kept intact. While fans pore over all of the minute differences and pick apart the changes, I’m just a little bit disappointed by how much doesn’t feel new. The combat is mostly the same. The enemies function very similarly. And the side content appears to be more or less the same from the original.

If I didn’t already know the contours of everything you can do in the game, I’m sure it would be thrilling to be discovering all of the arcane secrets and connections for the first time (my kids would rather play friendslop). But with no Master Quest mode at launch, or additional bonus dungeons that iterate on modern design sensibilities and all of the lessons Nintendo has taught players in the intervening 28 years, the overall experience has been a bit hollow for me.

Nintendo may be planning to add that type of thing in future DLC. I’m not sure there’s any benefit to waiting, especially when the magic of sweeping millennial nostalgia is in full force at launch. For now I’m delighted but conflicted. I’d recommend the Ocarina of Time remake as the best way to play the game without reservation. I just wish more of it felt like the first time playing Ocarina of Time. But that’s my problem, not the game’s.

Last Friday, the very good RPG Nier: Automata underwent what the scientific community calls 'puffer-fishification'. That is to say, it suddenly and inexplicably ballooned to over 700% of its original download size, as an update magically transformed the game from a reedy 50 GB lightweight into a 366-GB behemoth.

But it has since hit the Ozempic and is now back down to its regular size. "We have identified the cause and now resolved this issue," tweeted the official Nier X account earlier today. "Apologies for any inconvenience caused."

Notably absent from the bugfix notice was any explanation as to just what had happened. It might be academic at this point, but I for one would love to know how you accidentally make a game seven times bigger without anything changing.

Squenix is keeping schtum, but Rick Lane suggested, in our article last week about the bug, that it might have been related to Nier: Automata's DLC packages. "When selected for download on Steam, each DLC appears to have the same file size as the base game", he noted. "Weirdly though, this is only true for the initial download and installation. Once the game is installed, the file reportedly reverts to a more appropriate size of roughly 50 gigs." Indeed, the suggested temporary workaround for the issue (before Square Enix fixed it) was to uncheck all the DLCs when you installed the game.

So it sure seems like, somehow, the base files for the entire original game got attached to each individual DLC package. That might also explain why the size increase only applied to the downloads and not its install size—the whole game got downloaded eight times, but it was simply overwriting the base game files over and over.

That's just my speculation though, and without more news from Squenix, that's all we're gonna get. For now, enjoy being able to once again download 2B and 9S' adventures without having to start the download two weeks in advance.

Eli Lilly's obesity pill showed it can ease sleep apnea in a late-stage trial

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Lilly plans to submit Foundayo to the FDA for moderate-to-severe sleep apnea in adults with obesity or overweight by the end of 2026

Eli Lilly $LLY announced positive results on Monday from two late-stage trials of its obesity pill Foundayo in adults with obstructive sleep apnea, and the company plans to submit the drug to the U.S. Food and Drug Administration by the end of 2026.

Foundayo, also known as orforglipron, met the primary endpoint of the 52-week ATTAIN-OSA program by reducing the apnea-hypopnea index, the standard measure of sleep apnea severity, the company said. The results were presented at the American Heart Association Scientific Sessions 2026 and published in JAMA.

Participants had moderate-to-severe sleep apnea and obesity or overweight. In the study of 363 participants who did not use positive airway pressure (PAP) therapy, Foundayo reduced breathing interruptions by 21.1 events per hour, or 43.8%, from an average baseline of 50.7. Placebo reduced them by 4.3 events per hour. Of those on Foundayo, 31.5% reached remission or mild disease.

In the study of 349 participants who used PAP therapy, Foundayo reduced events by 18.3 per hour, or 27.5%, from an average baseline of 57.6. Of those participants, 19.6% reached remission or mild disease.

Foundayo cut sleep apnea-specific hypoxic burden, which gauges the depth and duration of oxygen drops during sleep, by 55.9% in participants not using PAP therapy and by 45.8% in those using it. Participants lost an average of 11.5% and 11.2% of their body weight, respectively, and systolic blood pressure fell by an average of 8.6 mmHg and 7.3 mmHg.

Lilly said all figures in its release represent the efficacy estimand, which reflects results had all randomized participants stayed on study treatment for 52 weeks. Figures reported by STAT for the group without PAP therapy were lower: a reduction of 17.8 events per hour on Foundayo versus 4.5 on placebo, with remission or mild disease in 28% of treated patients versus 13% on placebo.

The safety profile was consistent with earlier trials in the ACHIEVE and ATTAIN programs, Lilly said. The most common adverse events involved the digestive system, such as nausea, diarrhea, constipation, dyspepsia and vomiting, and ranged from mild to moderate. Discontinuation due to adverse events was 12.2% on Foundayo versus 3.8% on placebo without PAP therapy, and 6.8% versus 0.6% with PAP therapy.

"Foundayo is already making a difference for people living with obesity, and with plans to submit these results to the FDA by the end of the year, we could have the opportunity to help people living with OSA," said Thomas Seck, Lilly Cardiometabolic Health's senior vice president of product development, in a statement, according to CNBC.

Foundayo is approved in the U.S. for adults with obesity. Lilly had received a recommendation from a European Medicines Agency committee in October to approve the pill for obesity and type 2 diabetes. Lilly's injectable Zepbound is approved in the U.S. for moderate-to-severe sleep apnea in adults with obesity.

Sleep apnea affects an estimated 936 million adults worldwide, and most have never been diagnosed, according to Lilly.

Donna Kelce Sells Florida Condo for $485K—Less Than a Year After Major Makeover

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

NFL matriarch Donna Kelce has offloaded her newly renovated Florida condo for $485,000—less than a year after carrying out a complete overhaul of the pad.

In March, it was revealed that the 74-year-old mother of NFL stars Travis and Jason Kelce had embarked on a major overhaul of her Orlando abode, hiring a local company to replace multiple windows and doors throughout the two-bedroom property.

The project sparked an over-the-top response from fans of the famous football family, and led to Donna's sons calling out the reaction in an episode of their podcast, "New Heights," during which they mocked their mother over the work, joking that she was attempting to turn her condo into a "gilded palace."

However, the renovations seemed to make a big difference when it came to selling the home, with TMZ reporting that the property has found a new owner.

Donna has owned the 1,434-square-foot property since 2019, when she purchased the residence for $355,000. The condo, which was built in 2005, features a wraparound balcony, a den, library, home office, and workout room.

The mom of two previously spoke out about the renovation in a candid interview with Architectural Digest, telling the outlet that work was completed on her home—while setting the record straight about the changes that were made to the dwelling.

"It’s a lot of windows because I’m on the side of the building. So there’s like 16 windows, and they’re floor-length, and it was quite a project," she shared, before hitting back at reports that she'd needed to replace six doors during the renovation.

"The windows that are floor-length along the balcony, they call all of them doors, but they weren’t," she stated. "There were only two doors. But there were six of those [in total], and two of them had handles on them."

As for the viral response to her renovation news, Donna admitted that it "took [her] by surprise," adding: "I was kind of taken off guard."

At the time, Donna revealed she was looking forward to relaxing inside the updated space, explaining that she's happily settled into her routine in the property, where she lives alone, having divorced her former spouse, Ed, soon after their sons finished college.

Addressing her daily routine at home, the proud grandma said she begins each day with coffee, before figuring out what's on her agenda—and confirming whether or not it will require her to go to the airport.

Her children are also a big priority, she said, noting that, while they are both "so busy, it's almost impossible" to speak to them every day, she does her best to check in as often as she can.

"Especially in the offseason, I have no idea what city they’re in [or] where they’re going. But they’re always on my mind," she said.

She admitted that much of her relaxation time revolves around her favorite gadget in the house—her television—which she regularly eats her meals in front of, noting that she "doesn't really care what anybody thinks" about her habits, particularly because she lives alone.

"It connects you to the world. It allows you to relax. Not only that, but it’s important to have to learn about the news and what’s going on in the world," she added.

Donna said she will occasionally fall asleep with the TV on, but sets a timer to ensure that she isn't snoozing in front of the screen for longer than 20 minutes.

And while she seems fairly relaxed about her life at home, the mother of two admitted that she does have one very clear rule for guests who visit: no pets allowed on the furniture.

"I don’t have any pets. I think a person’s home is their home, and if that’s where they want their pet, have at it. If it’s in my house, no. But most people that I know of who have animals allow their pets on the furniture," she explained.

In addition to being a place Donna can relax, she said her condo was also packed with precious reminders of her sons' success—and the incredible experiences she has enjoyed as a result of their hard work.

“There are so many [photos]. So many pictures of all the amazing people that I've been able to meet, all the wonderful places that I've been able to go, all the games, all the Super Bowls,” Donna shared.

“It's more than a mother should ever hope for. I can't even tell you how full of love and admiration [I am] for my kids to be able to give that to our family.”

Donna's dwelling first shot into the headlines back in March, when TMZ revealed that she was carrying out construction work at the home. Her sons then added further fuel to the fire when they raised it as a topic of conversation on "New Heights."

Addressing the furor surrounding the renovations on their podcast that same week, Travis and Jason joked to their listeners: "Let's talk about the most important news out of the Kelce family this week. We wanted to break this news on the show, but reports have already started to leak out."

"Donna Kelce is remodeling her modest Florida home! Let's give it up for Donna!" Jason added.

The siblings then brought Donna in to share her thoughts on the media response to her renovation plans—with the NFL matriarch admitting that she had been stunned by the attention it received.

"Even TMZ sent me a text and said, 'Can you be honest, it's really funny!' I'm like ... it's so stupid!" she said, before attempting to play down the work she's having done.

"All they're doing is, people are coming to put in my windows on Monday," she continued. "You know my whole apartment is all windows. It's windows all the way around."

Still, Jason couldn't help but get in another joke, asking: "Are you going to bulletproof?"

Undeterred, Donna continued by explaining that the unseasonably cold weather in Orlando had motivated her to replace the existing window designs, because they were "cheap" and outdated, providing little protection against the chill.

"This year, it got so cold down here—it was in the 30s—and I literally had sweat coming down the windows and pools of water in my living room because it couldn't handle it. These are cheap windows that were put in 20 years ago," she shared.

Jason then called out TMZ's description of Donna's property as a "modest home," joking that she appears to have plans to turn it into a much swankier abode.

"Well, I just wanted to say, 'Mom, they said "modest home," and it just feels like you're turning this place into a bit of a gilded palace,'" he said.

One of the common misconceptions people have is that they think applying for credit cards will hurt their credit. The opposite is largely true, actually. During the last international break, during which the Three Lions completed four fixtures, Pickford and Trafford played in two each.

England are due to face Croatia and Spain in Nations League clashes over the next week and there could again be changes between the sticks.

Tuchel said: “Traff gets regular starts now, that’s what we wanted. James Trafford did well. They shared the matches last time. I’m not sure what we will do. There’s an opportunity that we maybe do this again.

“I have to speak with our goalkeeper coach. But the goalkeepers, they are a bit of a unit for themselves, and rightly so.

“It’s slightly different [to outfield players], but when do I have a chance to see another goalkeeper, if not in Nations League, if not in high-pressure matches?

“So, we had James against Spain, we asked ourselves, ‘is this fair for James Trafford to straightaway go against Spain?’.

“But I think it’s exactly what we need because what happens if Jordan Pickford is injured? What happens if he’s not available?

“So, it’s better to test it. Jordan knows about this. That we want to use Nations League for exactly that. But at the moment, nothing has changed. At the moment, he’s the number one and he just feels a bit of competition, which is good for him.”

Trafford made a £40m switch from Manchester City to Leeds in the summer and is benefiting from regular football.

He played second-fiddle to Gianluigi Donnarumma across the Pennines, yet now sits comfortably at the top of the pecking order in West Yorkshire.

Alongside Pickford and Trafford in the Three Lions squad is Brighton & Hove Albion’s Jason Steele, who is uncapped and appears unlikely to see any action.

England will face Croatia at Wembley on Thursday (November 12) and Spain in Madrid on Sunday (November 15).

Milan defender Strahinja Pavlovic is unlikely to join up with the Serbia national team squad for the November international break due to an ongoing issue with his kidney, which has kept him out of the last two matches at club level.

Pavlovic was one of six Serie A players, along with Vanja Milinkovic-Savic, Filip and Aleksandar Stankovic, Lazar Samardzic and Aleksa Terzic, originally summoned by Serbia head coach Veljko Paunovic for Nations League fixtures against Germany Greece coming up this week.

However, Pavlovic has missed the last two Milan matches with a kidney issue. He was not in the matchday squad for the 2-0 Europa League defeat against Ferencvaros on Thursday, nor the 2-1 win over Genoa in Serie A on Sunday.

According to reports from Gianluca Di Marzio, Pavlovic will not join up with his international colleagues this month, but Serbia head coach Paunovic has recently hinted that there is still a small possibility he joins at a later date, albeit an ‘unlikely’ scenario.

“Pavlovic is being monitored because of a kidney problem. We spoke this morning. He needs to have some tests. He might still join up, but it’s unlikely,” said Paunovic on Monday.

The premise of “Elf” is brilliant. Strategy Buys $82M in Bitcoin as STRC Tokenized Stock Adds New Twist to Saylor’s BTC

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Strategy Bitcoin holdings climbed to 850,364 BTC after Michael Saylor’s company purchased another 1,036 Bitcoin for $81.9 million. The latest acquisition came as Bitcoin traded below the company’s average purchase price, putting its enormous treasury under renewed market pressure.

According to a November 9 SEC filing, Strategy bought the Bitcoin between November 2 and 8 at an average price of $79,012 per coin. Its total acquisition cost now stands at $64.16 billion, with an average purchase price of $75,456.

Strategy has acquired 1,036 $BTC and repurchased $188M of $STRC. As of 11/8/26, we hold 850,364 BTC and $4.9B of USD Assets. $MSTR https://t.co/Pxqso5aVRU

The latest purchase also coincided with a $188.3 million repurchase of Strategy’s STRC preferred shares. The company funded both transactions through its common-stock offering program, highlighting how Michael Saylor continues using capital markets to finance its Bitcoin accumulation.

Strategy sold 2,051,348 shares of its Class A common stock through its at-the-market offering program during the reporting period. The sales generated $283.8 million in net proceeds. The company allocated then $81.9 million toward Bitcoin purchases, while $188.3 million went toward repurchasing 1,887,464 STRC preferred shares. Another $13.6 million was allocated to its general USD cash balance.

The latest transactions show how Strategy accumulation involves more than simply issuing shares and purchasing Bitcoin. The company is also actively managing its preferred securities and liquidity.

Strategy maintained approximately $4.77 billion in its USD Reserve and another $146.2 million in general USD Cash. This reserve supports preferred-stock dividends and interest payments on outstanding debt.

Meanwhile, Bitcoin’s decline below Strategy’s average acquisition price has put its treasury underwater on paper. At $74,000 per BTC, the company’s holdings would carry an unrealized loss exceeding $1 billion. But the latest purchase shows that Saylor has not backed away from accumulation despite the weaker market environment.

The $188.3 million repurchase also puts Strategy’s preferred securities back into the spotlight. STRC, known as Stretch, is a variable-rate perpetual preferred stock designed to trade around its $100 stated amount. It pays cash dividends, with its rate adjusted periodically to encourage price stability.

The instrument has also entered the tokenized securities market through Ondo Finance’s STRCon. Launched in April 2026, STRCon provides blockchain-based economic exposure to Strategy’s Stretch preferred stock. The token is available across Ethereum, Solana, and BNB Chain, subject to geographic and investor restrictions.

Tokenholders receive economic exposure and dividend-related benefits, but do not obtain conventional shareholder voting rights. This gives Strategy’s digital credit securities another distribution channel. Investors outside traditional brokerage platforms can access tokenized exposure through supported blockchain infrastructure.

However, tokenized trading does not generate fresh capital for Strategy automatically. Secondary-market transactions change ownership between investors and do not directly fund additional Bitcoin purchases. Still, this fits into Strategy’s longer-term ambition to build digital credit products for an increasingly tokenized financial system.

Strategy’s latest purchase arrived during a difficult period for institutional Bitcoin demand. US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $1.3 billion in net outflows last week. Last Friday alone accounted for $411.9 million in withdrawals.

BlackRock’s IBIT led Friday’s outflows with approximately $403 million in redemptions. Fidelity’s FBTC recorded $42.8 million in inflows, offering only a partial offset.

Bitcoin also fell toward $74,000 this morning, reaching its weakest level since August. The decline followed broader crypto selling pressure and growing concerns surrounding regulatory uncertainty as AI stocks slide.

The contrast between ETF withdrawals and Strategy’s continued accumulation highlights two different approaches to Bitcoin exposure. ETF investors can reduce exposure through daily redemptions, while Strategy continues using equity financing and its preferred-stock structure to expand its treasury.

The latest Bitcoin acquisition from Strategy reinforces the company’s long-term commitment. Strategy Bitcoin holdings now exceed 850,000 BTC, leaving the company with more than 4% of Bitcoin’s maximum supply.

Whether that commitment pays off will depend heavily on Bitcoin’s next market cycle, alongside Strategy’s ability to maintain access to capital markets.

A Bunch Of Psychologists Were Asked About You Can See Everything, And Their Answers Are Fascinating

You Can See Everything Has So Many Close-Ups Of Elizabeth Holmes’ Face. Nathan Fielder Explains Why

You Can See Everything is the most talked-about movie on the 2026 movie schedule, and for good reason. There are many WTF moments that people have latched on to. There is one, it seems, that was left on the cutting room floor that Billy Evans just told the world about via X. It turns out, according to Evans, Elizabeth Holmes’ partner, got into a wrestling match with co-director Nathan Fielder. As wild as the movie got, this was not something I expected would happen, nor would I have expected it not to make the final cut.

Fielder is known for doing a lot of, let’s say, unconventional stuff in his projects, and that certainly holds true in You Can See Everything. Evans, for his part, seems to have been all in on going for it, too. There’s that now-infamous scene of Evans in the shower, a scene that he claims wasn’t his idea, disputing the claims of co-director Lance Oppenheim. I really wish they'd kept the scene where I wrestled Nathan, Borat style. Full top mount. His little Canadian frame struggling beneath me. I thought he was in on the joke. Until he wasn't. Imagine realizing that in camera.

Not only that, but according to Evans, Fielder ended up in the pool at the rented beach house where Evans and Holmes lived in the weeks leading up to Holmes’ imprisonment on fraud convictions and where most scenes with Evans and Holmes were filmed. Evans explains in a follow-up tweet after being asked if the two of them were naked:

Clothed. But Lance Oppenheim asked him to: 1) stand by the edge of the pool 2) remove his phone and monitor 3) look up Then I tossed Nathan into the pool. Borat wrestling ensued. I thought it was a bit. It wasn't.

He doesn’t go further into how it wasn’t a bit, or that Fielder was surprised by it. Honestly, knowing Fielder, I’d be pretty surprised myself if Fielder wasn’t in on it with Oppenheim. I guess anything is possible.

Evans also teased that there is a lot more that didn’t make the movie, saying in another tweet:

This literally wouldn't make the top 10 best deleted scenes. We didn't hold back. We showed them everything. You saw what they wanted to show you.... It was a wild experience. Hate us, [love] us, the truth or lack thereof is in the edit.

It’s hard to imagine that a director’s cut with added scenes could be better than the theatrical cut that we’ve all seen now, but if Fielder and Evans wrestling isn’t one of the top 10 best scenes left out, then I really hope the home release of You Can See Everything includes a lot more! Meanwhile, the film is closing in on a $100 million haul at the domestic box office, and that is just as astonishing as anything left out of the movie.

Michael Carrick is ‘clear’ about his plans as he clings onto Man Utd job

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United go into the international break in the Premier League relegation zone after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick remains confident in his ability to oversee Manchester United’s resurgence after a “right mixed” three weeks left his team in the relegation zone and the head coach’s future under scrutiny.

The 20-time league champions will spend the November international break in 18th place as their wait to add to August’s solitary Premier League win against Ipswich continued against Aston Villa.

United showed spirit and all too familiar flaws as Sunday’s helter-skelter clash ended in a 4-4 draw after substitutes Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu scored in stoppage time following Tuesday’s 3-2 comeback win against Roma.

Carrick insisted afterwards that he would not walk away from a job that looked like it could be taken from him a fortnight ago and the international break provides an opportunity for the club to take stock.

Asked if the two-week pause comes at a good time for the group, the under-fire United boss said: “Yeah, it’s been a right mixed week or two or three, really, if you’re looking at the events.

“But I’m pretty clear where we need to go and how we need to get there.

“Obviously some of the boys will be flying off around the world and different things, and the group will be split for 10 days or so.

“But as a normal international week is, you know, we’ll have some training. We’ll have one or two days off, but not too much. But we’ll train, and we’ll get ready for the next game.”

The final international break of the year would be a logical time for United to make a change given there is no free midweek until mid-December after returning with a trip to rivals Liverpool on November 22.

But Carrick is steadfast in his belief that he can lead the embattled side up the standings having seen enough positives since a 4-1 defeat at Como and 3-0 home loss to Bournemouth left his job hanging by a thread.

United failed to take their chances and saw Lisandro Martinez sent off in a 2-0 loss at Chelsea, before Tuesday’s stirring 3-2 Champions League comeback against Roma was followed by the dramatic Villa draw.

“Goals change games, and you’ve got to deal with it, and it’s part of something we have to improve on drastically,” Carrick said.

“But there’s more than enough. We’re just making it too difficult for ourselves at the moment in certain times.”

Carrick is far from perfect but has been let down by United’s transfer business in the view of outspoken defender Martinez and the fans who frequently aim chants at director of football Jason Wilcox.

Left-back was among the areas of need and Luke Shaw’s injury has seen Diogo Dalot often deployed there, but frustrations with the United full-back reached boiling point on Sunday as supporters cheered his substitution.

Head coach Carrick, who was unaware of United’s full-back then being hounded as he left the ground, said: “We’ve got to be careful with that, I think.

“I understand the frustration. I understand the disappointment of the supporters, so I get it. We’re all frustrated. We’re all disappointed.

“The players are as much as anyone, I guarantee you that. Diogo gives absolutely everything. He’s played in different positions. He’s played left-back. He’s played really well at times.

“He gives a penalty away. He knows that, maybe he can do a little bit better. Alright, that’s part of it, he’s made a mistake.

“But he cares an awful lot, and he’s given everything, so I like what he brings to the team. I like what he brings to the group.”

This prototype camera is fighting the good fight against AI photography

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

If video killed the radio star, then AI slop returned the favor. At least, that is what Josh Cowell, founder of camera maker CAIM1, will tell you. Short for Counter Artificial Intelligence Machine 1, the startup is working to reestablish our collective faith in the validity of images through its latest prototype: a camera that embeds cryptographic proof of a photo's authenticity directly into the image. As Cowell described in a blog post by the startup's silicon chip provider, Tropic Square, "the traditional photograph is dead, and designing a secure camera to replace it is an exercise in healthy paranoia."

When you take a photo using its prototype, CAIM1's internal chip creates a unique silicon fingerprint that certifies when, where, and with which camera the photo was taken. This verification is then tied to a unique, anonymous blockchain signature, creating an auditable pipeline for researchers and general viewers to authenticate.

Damaging deepfakes have proliferated across our digital sphere. In a report by the European Parliament, social media deepfakes have doubled every six months since 2023, reaching an estimated 8 million in 2025. If current trajectories hold, Europol projects that 90 percent of online content will be AI-generated by the end of the year. Meanwhile, firms like xAI, Google, Meta and Apple have come under legal fire for neglecting to rein AI-sparked disinformation. Lax safeguards have enabled the systematic spread of nonconsensual sexual deepfakes, with Europol estimating that 98 percent of deepfakes were pornographic.

CAIM1 hopes its camera will help combat the trend. Still in the prototype phase, CAIM1 expects its flagship camera to be released sometime in the first quarter of 2027. According to founder Josh Cowell, "CAIM1 is ultimately just the foundation of a comprehensive, verifiable infrastructure we are developing designed to aid digital truth."

CAIM1's authentication process begins with its silicon chip, the Tropic01, which Tropic Square describes on its website as the "world's first auditable secure element." When CAIM1 snaps a photo, light photons pass through a "digital vault" on their way to its sensors, creating hardware-level verification embedded directly in the image.  Because of irregularities in the silicon chip's manufacturing process, no two digital fingerprints are the same, ensuring that each signature is wholly unique.

Once captured, verification is stored on what CAIM1 describes as an "immutable decentralized infrastructure," creating an unchangeable, auditable, anonymous record verifying its validity. To protect users' information, CAIM1 encrypts this sensitive data through advanced physical noise systems and internal encryption methods, deploying digital tripwires and advanced encryption to prevent outside access via stolen encryption keys or brute force attacks.

Of course, CAIM1 isn't the only company investing in hardware-led photographic verification. Apple for instance, launched hardware attestation for its iPhone 18 Pro and 18 Pro Max's through its Reference Image system. Sony, Canon and Nikon, meanwhile, announced a joint initiative to embed digital signatures in their camera's photographs in 2024.

However, CAIM1's founder explained in a blog post by Tropic Square that the company's cameras are more transparent than its competitors, as Tropic Square's chips allow both researchers and the general public to "audit its internal architecture." Comparatively, competitors require "users to place blind faith in a single corporate entity's closed ecosystem," said Cowell.

The camera can also create a physical receipt for every image. The paper includes a permanent QR code to access the photo, a blockchain reference number, critical information such as the date, time, and location of the photograph, and a unique access code enabling digital access to the photograph.

In building CAIM1, developers needed to find a means of reducing the heat caused by its processors, as excessive temperatures can degrade picture quality. Unfortunately, traditional heat reduction methods like heatsinks couldn't combat the extreme temperatures generated by the camera's advanced processors, while while most fans were too noisy and created micro vibrations that could cause motion blur.

To solve these issues, CAIM1 partnered with PC fan manufacturer Noctua to install a unique active-cooling airflow system. Dubbed the NF-A4x10, the unified single-airstream design pulls cool air across the camera's two major heat sources, its CPU and sensor, while expelling excess heat. Two anti-vibration mounts prevent blurring and ensure picture integrity.

Of course, no one cares if your picture is authentic if the quality sucks. Capable of hi-resolution 4K video, CAIM1 claims that its camera is designed with high octane action sports in mind. As the startup wrote in a blog post, "We didn't set out to build a gimmick or a toy; the CAIM1 is designed from the ground up as a high performance, rugged camera that videographers can rely on in extreme, fast paced environments."

To date, however, little is known about the actual specifications of the camera. CAIM1's website touts its Bring-Your-Own-Lens design, which allows customers to upgrade their camera with either vintage or modern lenses. In recent tests, the startup attached two premium glass lenses from Japanese camera company Kowa Optronics' JC10M series and showcased the results at Germany's infamous Nürburgring racetrack. If the results are anything to judge by, CAIM1 is more than merely an authentication tool. For more information on the company's efforts to combat deepfakes, you can checkout its website, blog, or social media.

Michael Carrick’s future is up to the Man United hierarchy – will they stick or twist?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

United’s recent ramshackle play has left them in the Premier League relegation zone and provided no definitive answer on Carrick’s capability, leaving the club’s decision makers with a big choice

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

As Patrick Dorgu rose highest in a crowded six-yard box to head in a 96th-minute equaliser, Omar Berrada clenched his fists in celebration and Jason Wilcox applauded. Normally, when the cameras pan to them, Manchester United’s chief executive looks on the verge of tears and their sporting director seems to be trying to disappear into zipped-up tops. Not this time. Their joy was probably tinged with relief but perhaps also confusion.

The decision-makers still face another decision. And if the fact Michael Carrick was given extra time to turn their season around may have been because they were paralysed by indecision rather than convinced he represented the solution, Carrick’s last fortnight has not given a definitive answer either way.

His side have not been as spineless and wretched as they were against either Como or Bournemouth. United’s last two matches have brought a victory and a draw. They have also conceded nine in the last three. The faith Wilcox, Berrada – and, by extension, Sir Jim Ratcliffe and the Glazers – showed in Carrick has been rewarded in parts of the games against Chelsea, Roma and Aston Villa, but certainly not over the full 90 minutes of any. Sunday’s 4-4 draw against Villa, following Tuesday’s 3-2 win over Roma, seemed to underline that United are not a serious football team any more. Indeed, ragged and ramshackle, there are times when they are barely a team at all.

And yet, with Carrick imperilled, they have summoned something from within, twice in five days. Maybe they are playing to save the manager’s skin.

But the precedents also included United’s previous 4-4 draw, 11 months ago against Bournemouth and in the final weeks of Ruben Amorim’s reign. Were they playing for him, or just in a game that took on a life of its own? There was also the example of Amorim’s 5-4 win over Lyon; like Carrick’s triumph over Roma, the craziness of it was scarcely a blueprint for future success.

So Wilcox and Berrada have much to ponder. When they opted not to sack Carrick two weeks ago, perhaps it was a case of timing, that their succession planning was not sufficiently advanced to move. They may have been put off by the Fletcher precedent: United’s former players tended to have excellent records as caretaker managers until Darren Fletcher found it tougher in January. Wilcox and Berrada might have found themselves without an ideal interim or permanent replacement.

Now an international break provides the chance to make a change. The onus switches to United’s powerbrokers, even if they again opt not to act. Carrick has confirmed he will not quit. “That’s not going to happen,” he said. Sacking him could make unpopular figures even more unpopular; or it could save the crowd from turning on an Old Trafford favourite. In the triangle of players, manager and hierarchy, only Carrick has had the vocal backing of the Stretford End of late. Carrick may have credit in the bank with the money men as well: by transforming last season and taking United into the Champions League, he made the club £100m.

But he has also taken them into the relegation zone in November. In itself, that could be deemed a sackable offence. Indeed, United have made their worst start in 40 years, when they also took eight points from their first 10 games, when they sacked their manager in November. But Ron Atkinson left them one place above the bottom three; they dropped into it after Sir Alex Ferguson’s first game, a 2-0 defeat at Oxford.

United even had more league wins at this stage in 1973-74, in the season when they went on to be relegated, when, at Christmas, their joint top scorer was the goalkeeper, Alex Stepney.

And for now, United are in danger of the drop. When Amorim talked of them being in a relegation battle in December 2024, they were not really; they had enough of a cushion. Not now; while December could offer some respite, containing meetings with the rest of the current bottom five, the risk is that United’s plight worsens before then.

There is no quick fix, no easy answers. Maybe United’s dismal defence will have more solidity when Luke Shaw is finally fit again. There was certainly the sense that the returning players could prove to be reinforcements. The fit-again Dorgu, who scored in Carrick’s first two games in January, might have scored in his last or extended his tenure. There are signs that, after so many were out of form, a couple of Carrick’s squad are belatedly showing something resembling their best, as Matheus Cunha has in the last two matches. But there are others who have got still worse.

So United need someone to transform their season in the way Carrick himself did last year, even if it is only the temporary boost a new-manager bounce can provide. But they can’t sack Michael Carrick and then appoint Michael Carrick. Do Wilcox and Berrada stick or twist? If the outside world does not know, perhaps they don’t either.
