That’s a Mora: Rodrigo and Roma rise with Lazio to give Eternal City new life

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Roma and Lazio have both made unbeaten starts to the Serie A season, giving those in the Italian capital dreams of a Scudetto

After nine games in 28 days, Gian Piero Gasperini finally got a moment to pause and reflect on Sunday night. The Roma manager was back in Liguria, where he worked for the best part of a decade across two stints at Genoa, to collect an award from Stelle Nello Sport – an association formed to promote sport in the region – celebrating his career achievements and commitment to courageous attacking football.

The timing worked out well. Gasperini’s Roma thumped Sassuolo 3-0 on Saturday, bouncing back from a midweek loss to Manchester United. That result meant the Giallorossi had scored 31 times in 11 Serie A games. In the last 65 years only two Italian teams – the Juventus and Napoli sides of 2017-18 – had ever made a more prolific start to a top-flight campaign. More importantly, Roma’s victory meant they remained undefeated in domestic competition this season. Just like their neighbours Lazio, who drew 1-1 away to Napoli in Sunday’s early kick-off.

Roma Caput Mundi? The old Latin phrase positions Rome as the capital of the world. Probably a little bit of a stretch, given Roma’s result against United. Lazio are not even competing in European competition this season and their own manager, Gennaro Gattuso, keeps claiming his priority is to avoid relegation.

Still, this is an extraordinary moment for football in the capital. According to Opta, this is the first time two teams from the same city have ever made it through 11 games of a Serie A season without a single defeat. When you consider that Rome’s two leading clubs have won just five Scudetti between them – as compared to 40 combined for Internazionale and Milan – the statistic jumps out even more.

Acknowledging this detail in an interview after collecting his award, Gasperini could not help but draw a contrast between his team and their rivals. “It’s a bit of a surprise for everyone to have this Scudetto derby situation playing out in November,” he said. “But it’s a reflection of the values of the city. “Rome is an extraordinary city which has a huge enthusiasm for football. For Roma the stadium is always full. Lazio might have some other problems. But the passion for football in Rome is incredible.”

His reference was to the mass boycott of Lazio’s home games by supporters, the reasons for which have been covered in recent editions of this column. Attendance figures for the Biancocelesti’s home games at the Stadio Olimpico this season hover at about 10,000 people. Roma drew 64,674 for Sassuolo on Saturday – in line with their average. Ticket sales do not decide football games. Lazio sit top of the table heading into the international break. But Roma have plenty to feel good about too.

Sassuolo were about the best team they could have faced this weekend. Seeking a bounce-back after their collapse in Manchester, Roma lined up against opponents who had earned a single point from their previous six games and owned the leakiest defence in the division.

Leonardo Balerdi had a header cleared off the line before Mario Hermoso put Roma ahead in the 27th minute, punishing some indecisive defending. Moments later, a scuffed shot from Donyell Malen – bobbling far off target – took deviations off a pair of Sassuolo players and wound up in the back of the net. The only question left was how many goals Roma would add. In the end it was just one, Rodrigo Mora cutting in from the left and swapping passes with Malen before striking back across his body into the bottom corner from the right side of the box.

A third consecutive Serie A game with a goal for the 19-year-old, who also set up Matías Soulé’s opener against United. And the Portuguese even played the initial ball to create the chance that led to their penalty in that game as well.

Gasperini has never been shy in his enthusiasm for Mora. “We’re standing before an extraordinary talent,” said the manager after including the teenager in his starting XI for a season-opening 4-0 win over Fiorentina in August. “Every time he touches the ball something beautiful happens.”

is not alone in noticing. Mora was called up by Portugal this week for their Nations League games against Denmark and Wales. He has trained with the senior team before but not played for them. There will be greater opportunities to do so now Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international football, though Mora expressed regret, too, at not getting to train again with a man he described as “my idol”.

Back at Roma, Gasperini has tried not to ask too much, too soon, of Mora, giving him a full 90 minutes only once, in a Champions League rout of Slovan Bratislava. Otherwise, he has alternated starts with opportunities from the bench during this busy early-season stretch.

Only in the last week did that change. Mora started against Udinese, scoring the second goal in a 5-2 win, then was back in the lineup to face United. Gasperini’s decision to take him off at half-time of that game has been widely criticised. These things are always easy to oversimplify, but it is true that Roma’s attacking threat seemed to vanish with Mora missing after the interval.

Venezia 2-1 Udinese, Torino 1-1 Lecce, Cagliari 1-0 Frosinone, Parma 1-2 Bologna, Roma 3-0 Sassuolo, Napoli 1-1 Lazio, Monza 0-1 Atalanta, Genoa 1-2 Milan, Inter 2-0 Como, Fiorentina 2-2 Juventus

Gasperini spoke at the end of that defeat of it being a lesson that Roma could learn from. His team is still a work in progress, but the trend lines are more than encouraging. Roma secured their Champions League spot by winning six of their final seven games last season. They have not lost a domestic home game for more than 11 months and even their most recent away defeat in Serie A, to Inter, was all the way back in April.

Mora’s rapid ascent has added a new wrinkle. The attack we have seen in Roma’s last three games, with him and Soulé playing either side of Malen in a 3-4-2-1 has carved opponents open with such devastating effectiveness that you could start to wonder whether a healthy Paulo Dybala – for so long the standout talent here – would displace any of them from a first-choice XI.

This is not really a debate anyone needs to have. Roma hope to get Dybala back from his calf injury after the international round, but his body has always imposed breaks for him in every season and the Giallorossi will need rotation in any case to keep everyone fresh through a long campaign.

The improved depth of Roma’s squad, on the other hand, is a reason to believe that they can sustain their strong start. Gasperini, like most managers, would prefer to talk about the next game than discuss title ambitions this early in a campaign, but when a reporter asked if he would sign up now for a guaranteed second place he dismissed the idea out of hand.

“We don’t need to give ourselves false illusions, but we’re not going to knock ourselves down for no reason either,” he said. “I don’t know what we can achieve.”

Scrambled Eggs Are 10x Creamier & Fluffier With One Boxed Ingredient

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

When you make scrambled eggs, heat, fat content, and your tools often require you to make them either fluffy or creamy, not both. However, powdered milk is an interesting ingredient that can help you bridge the divide, imparting richness and enabling volume, provided you use it correctly.

Powdered milk can be made from skim milk, whole milk, and buttermilk, but all are essentially just milk with the water taken out and converted into a soluble powder. When it comes to water versus milk in scrambled eggs, folks typically favor the latter because it gives a bit of extra richness and flavor. However, it can weigh them down, unlike water, which steams to fluff up the little curds into pillowy softness. While you can't just add water to powdered milk eggs because you'd just be using milk with extra steps, it does allow you to employ other tricks that increase the fluffiness, giving you the best of both worlds.

Powdered milk is also quite a bit more convenient since it's shelf-stable and dry. Not only does it last longer than regular milk, but you can also toast the powder to lend a nutty savoriness similar to browned butter. Roughly a tablespoon per three eggs is all you need to achieve serious flavor. Nonfat varieties typically last much longer than full-fat varieties, which tend to have a shorter shelf life. Freezing it can extend its shelf life even further, so it's an easy and cheap ingredient to keep on hand.

The biggest benefit of powdered versus liquid milk is that it adds milk solids without adding extra water. This helps you avoid making your scrambled eggs too watery and is why, while it may not make them fluffy on its own, it also won't inhibit other tricks. There's no shortage of expert tips for restaurant-quality eggs, but try focusing on ones that add a bit of extra air and/or cook them quickly for the best results.

You'll want to thoroughly incorporate your powdered milk, but luckily, a thorough whisking also helps introduce some air into the eggs. The goal is to make your mixture paler and more voluminous, looking as if you added extra liquid. The mason jar hack of shaking your eggs is not only easier than learning a fancy technique, but also helps ensure your powder is dispersed properly. If you're making a ton, you can even repurpose a protein shaker bottle and let that little metal whisk ball do the heavy lifting for you.

Slowly bringing your eggs to temperature is a French style that gives them a creamy, porridge-like consistency. However, this also slowly coagulates the proteins and prevents them from fluffing up properly. The key is to cook them fast enough that their natural moisture steams, but not so hot that they turn rubbery. Full-fat milk powder can help contribute to a tender texture and, when used in conjunction with moderate heat, help produce eggs that are moist but fluffy, creamy but firm.

Next Crystal Palace manager: Contenders to replace Pierre Sage as clear favourite emerges

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Pierre Sage will be sacked as Crystal Palace manager following Sunday’s 3-1 home defeat by Liverpool.

The Frenchman’s job has been under serious scrutiny for weeks now, with Palace heading into the November international break sitting rock bottom of the Premier League table after winning just two and losing seven of their first 10 games this season.

Sage has struggled to get his ideas across since taking over from Oliver Glasner in the summer, having enjoyed a terrific campaign with Lens last term.

The Eagles have the worst defence in the Premier League with 24 goals conceded so far, and Sage has run out of time to convince Palace’s decision-makers that he is the right man for the job.

With an announcement on Sage’s future imminent, they have already sounded out potential replacements.

Standard Sport has assessed who could take over at Selhurst Park next...

Out of work since being sacked by Tottenham in February, Thomas Frank was one of several candidates that Palace looked at to succeed Glasner in the summer and is the early frontrunner to replace Sage.

Crystal Palace set to sack Sage with club bottom of Premier League

Frank tipped to be next Crystal Palace manager as new rival emerges

When is FA Cup draw? Start time, ball numbers and TV channel today

Frank’s reputation is in need of rehabilitation, and it is unclear how much of a toll his torrid eight-month spell in charge of Spurs took, but he remains highly regarded after his work with Brentford.

Frank released a statement in June saying he was taking some time out of football before his next job, so the Dane may be unwilling to jump into a role so soon after the season has started.

Another manager who stepped away from football over the summer, Kieran McKenna made the shock decision to leave Ipswich after earning promotion back to the Premier League.

One of Britain’s most highly sought-after young coaches, McKenna’s decision to spend more time with his family came as a surprise, but potentially hints at the fact that he is not yet ready to get back into management.

McKenna’s patient, possession-based style would constitute a dramatic change in tactics for Palace, but the Northern Irishman is comfortable using a three-man defence when his team has the ball.

Bringing in former Newcastle boss Eddie Howe would be considered quite the coup for Palace.

The Englishman has attracted interest from Manchester United amid ongoing doubts over Michael Carrick’s future and guided Newcastle to Carabao Cup glory in 2025.

Howe is actively open to new opportunities, but is said to be willing to wait for an elite, high-profile job, which could leave Palace out of the running.

Arguably the most divisive name on the list, Sean Dyche’s pragmatic approach is a far cry from the expansive, counter-attacking football Palace fans have been used to under Glasner and, to a lesser extent, Sage.

Dyche’s Premier League experience, especially given that Palace will have one eye on ensuring they stay in the top-flight, makes him an attractive proposition for those in the boardroom.

Whether they would be prepared to face the inevitable backlash his appointment would prompt is a different question entirely, though.

Coventry boss Frank Lampard is reportedly under consideration for the Palace job despite signing only a new contract with the promoted Sky Blues in June.

Lampard is a long-standing target and one of several other candidates Palace are reportedly eyeing up alongside Kim Hellberg and Vitor Matos, who have led Middlesbrough and Swansea, respectively, towards the top of the Championship table this term.

Free agent Vitor Pereira is also said to be on Palace’s shortlist, while Australia head coach and former Palace captain Tony Popovic could be a surprise contender.

England assistant Anthony Barry is a risky but potentially intriguing appointment, while former Wolves boss Rob Edwards is well known to Palace sporting director Matt Hobbs.

Dashboard Confessional Announce 2027 Unplugged: 25th Anniversary Tour

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Dashboard Confessional will celebrate the 25th anniversary of their seminal Unplugged performance with a 15-date North American tour. The trek kicks off in Chicago on March 12 and continues through April 3, when it wraps in San Francisco. It will hit cities like New York, Boston, Nashville, and Los Angeles.

“Dashboard Confessional’s Unplugged was a pivotal and unifying moment for the band and our fans,” frontman Chris Carrabba said in a statement. “I’m so excited to celebrate that moment in time. I’ve recently done some thrillingly small solo acoustic shows in Nashville, New York and Los Angeles — the energy around those shows was, fittingly, electric. I’m excited to carry that spirit into bigger venues with the band arrangements from our original Unplugged performance.”

Dashboard Confessional performed on MTV’s Unplugged series on April 24, 2002. It was released as an album, MTV Unplugged 2.0, that December, a year ahead of their 2003 breakout LP, A Mark, a Mission, a Brand, a Scar.

Ticket information for the tour is available on the band’s website. The Saints & Sailors Fan Club presale begins Nov. 12 at 9 a.m. local. Prediction: Ivory Coast vs Gambia

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ivory Coast and Gambia will meet at Stade de la Paix in Bouake on Thursday, November 12, as the two early leaders in Group C continue their qualification campaigns for the 2027 Africa Cup of Nations.

Both teams have collected six points from their opening two matches. Ivory Coast has done so without conceding, while Gambia arrives after scoring six goals against Somalia and Ghana.

Verdict: Over 2.5 goals Best odds: 16/25 Bookmaker: 1xBet

Ivory Coast has made a convincing start to qualifying, beating Ghana and Somalia by the same 2-0 scoreline. Those results have put the Elephants in a strong position before a pair of matches against their closest challenger.

Gambia has already won away to Ghana and has enough attacking quality to cause problems if the home side commits too many players forward.

Still, Ivory Coast has several ways to stretch the visiting defense. Amad Diallo, Nicolas Pepe and Yan Diomande give Renard options out wide, where beating the first defender could force Gambia’s central players out of position.

That should be an important part of the home side’s approach. Moving the ball quickly between the wings would make it harder for Gambia to protect the middle and give Ivory Coast opportunities to deliver into the penalty area.

The Elephants won the most recent meeting 1-0 in March 2025. Their opening qualifying results also suggest they can manage a game without allowing much at the other end.

However, Gambia’s attacking form gives this meeting a different feel. Ivory Coast may need to keep looking for goals even after taking the lead.

Renard has confirmed that Amad Diallo is fit following the injury concern that forced him off during his club’s match on October 31.

Odilon Kossounou and Guela Doue are absent from the squad. Evan Ndicka, Ousmane Diomande and Emmanuel Agbadou remain available in central defense, while Oumar Solet is among the new additions to the group.

Franck Kessie and Ibrahim Sangare offer experience in midfield, with Malick Yalcouye another option.

Further forward, Renard can choose from Amad, Pepe, Yan Diomande, Bazoumana Toure and Martial Godo, alongside central attacking options Elye Wahi and Bonny.

There is enough variety to change the attack during the match, particularly if Gambia manages to close the spaces around its penalty area.

Gambia’s opening results deserve attention. The Scorpions began with a 2-1 victory over Somalia before producing a 4-2 comeback win away to Ghana.

That second performance showed how dangerous this team can be when a match opens up. Gambia trailed 2-0 in Accra before Mohamed Badamosi and Omar Colley brought it level before halftime. Ebrima Colley then scored twice after the break to complete the turnaround.

Recovering from that position should give Johnathan McKinstry’s players confidence heading into another difficult away assignment. They have already shown that falling behind does not have to end their chances.

The defensive side of those performances is worth considering, though. Gambia conceded against Somalia and allowed Ghana to establish a two-goal lead. Similar lapses in Bouake could prove expensive.

McKinstry will probably want his midfield to stay close to the defense while leaving enough support for counterattacks. Sitting too deep for long periods would invite repeated pressure from Ivory Coast’s wide players.

There should be opportunities to break when the hosts push forward. Whether Gambia can use those moments effectively may determine how competitive this match becomes.

Gambia has been boosted by the return of Musa Barrow and Yankuba Minteh, who missed the opening two qualifiers through injury.

Their availability gives McKinstry additional attacking options alongside Ebrima Colley and Badamosi, both of whom contributed to the successful start.

However, Jesper Ceesay and Suleiman Camara have subsequently withdrawn because of injuries. Saidou Khan and Salieu Drammeh have been called up as their replacements.

Alieu Fadera is also unavailable with a thigh injury. Omar Colley offers experience in defense, while Ebou Adams and Abubakarr Barry are among the midfield options.

The returning attackers should help Gambia carry a threat beyond its own half. McKinstry will need that outlet if his team spends long stretches defending.

Ivory Coast should create enough chances to put Gambia’s defense under sustained pressure. The visitors have conceded in both qualifiers, and recovering from another poor start would be difficult against this opposition.

Gambia also has reasons to believe it can contribute to the scoring. Six goals in two qualifiers, followed by the return of Barrow and Minteh, give the away side a credible attacking threat.

Another controlled 2-0 home win is the main concern for this selection. Even so, the attacking options available and Gambia’s defensive vulnerabilities make over 2.5 goals the preferred bet.

Verdict: Over 2.5 goals Best odds: 16/25 Bookmaker: 1xBet

Les U16 n'ont pas fait de détail

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Après leur déconvenue d'il y a trois semaines, et la défaite face au CASCO Oullins à l'extrême limite du temps additionnel (3-2), les U16 de Kevin De Jesus avaient à coeur de réagir. La trêve avait été meublée par une rencontre amicale à Bourg-Peronnas remportée par 3 à 0 (buts de N.Fontaine et Joris Rochette 2), histoire de garder le rythme et de parfaire les automatismes. Les Verts se présentaient hier, sur le terrain synthétique Georges Bereta, avec une équipe dans laquelle figuraient quatre U15 (Ilan Clapeyron, Riad Moutae, Kaïs Boukerma et Hugo Durin) face à la lanterne rouge, l'AFCA (Aurillac Foot Cantal Auvergne) qui, il faut bien l'avouer, n'a pas opposé une farouche résistance aux Verts. Ces derniers l'ont en effet emporté sur le large score de 9 à 0, alors qi'ils menaient déjà 4-0 au repos.

Ce match a ressemblé à une attaque-défense, digne d'un match de handball. Dès la 2ème minute, Hugo Durin se mettait déjà en évidence avec un tir au ras du poteau. Dans la foulée, à la réception d'un ballon venu de la droite, il éliminait son adversaire et marquait  d'un tir au ras du sol. (ASSE-AFCA : 1-0, 3ème).Le jeu se déroulait devant le but aurillacois et Rayan Fontaine, servi par Adem Moussaoui, d'un tir dans un angle fermé aggravait le score. (ASSE-AFCA : 2-0, 7ème)La domination verte ne se relâchait pas : sur un corner, la reprise de la tête de Raphaël Moser était détournée par le gardien (17ème), un coup-franc de Lenny Nebout, tiré à ras de terre, était repoussé par le portier cantalou et la reprise de Noah Fontaine ne trouvait pas le cadre (18ème), un tir de loin de Dixon Ahamadi était une nouvelle fois détourné (22ème). Entre temps, une première incursion aurillacoise était ponctuée par un corner, mais la reprise de la tête n,'inquiétait pas Ilan Clapeyron.  Les Verts allaient de nouveau trouver la faille : à la suite d'une combinaison avec Noah Fontaine, Hugo Durin était trouvé dans un petit espace et sa frappe croisée du gauche faisait mouche. (ASSE-AFCA : 3-0, 24ème).Dès la remise en jeu, le défenseur central Idris Balta s'avançait, plaçait un tir qui rebondissait devant le but et trompait le gardien adverse. (ASSE-AFCA : 4-0, 25ème).A la 30ème minute, suite à un centre de Raphaël Moser, Hugo Durin remisait sur Joris Rochette (photo), mais la reprise de ce dernier était contrée. Aurillac réagissait timidement à la 38ème et sur un tir venu de loin, Ilan Clapeyron se détendait bien. C'était un petit intermède au milieu de la domination stéphanoise qui ne se démentait pas : un coup-franc de Rayan Fontaine trouvait la tête de Dixon Ahamadi, mais le gardien adverse captait le cuir (40ème), une frappe lointaine de Joris Rochette s'écrasait sur l'équerre du but cantalou (43ème), sur une combinaison côté gauche, le tir de Raphaël Moser dans un angle fermé frôlait la transversale (44ème), et, enfin à la 45ème, un tir dévié de Joris Rochette, manquait de tromper le gardien. La mi-temps était sifflée sur ces entrefaites. Le score de 4 à 0 illustrait la mainmise stéphanoise.

Le second acte repartait avec la même physionomie. A la 50ème une subtile combinaison Joris Rochette - Hugo Durin butait sur le gardien. A la 52ème, Joris Rochette repiquait au centre et sa frappe du gauche passait à côté. La minute suivante Hugo Durin alertait Rayan Fontaine et le centre de ce dernier trouvait la tête de son frère Noah, mais sa reprise était détournée en corner, corner sur lequel la tête de Dixon Ahamadi ne trouvait pas le cadre. A la 55ème, Rayan Fontaine était fauché à l'entrée de la surface et le coup-franc de Raphaël Moser s'écrasait sur la transversale aurillacoise. A force d'appuyer, les Verts allaient trouver la faille : une frappe de 25 mètres de Dixon Ahamadi était détournée par le gardien et sur le corner qui suivait Rayan Fontaine trouvait la tête d'Hugo Durin. (ASSE-AFCA : 5-0, 56ème) A la 62ème, Hugo Durin servait latéralement Lenny Nebout mais la frappe de ce dernier était stoppée par le gardien. Servi par une passe verticale d'Idris Balta, Kaïs Boukerma voyait son tir détourné par le portier aurillacois (65ème). A la 69ème, Rayan Fontaine trouvait Hugo Durin dans le dos de la défense et ce dernier concluait du droit. (ASSE-AFCA : 6-0, 69ème).Peu après, Hugo Durin, alerté par Joris Rochette, partait de la ligne médiane, prenait de vitesse la défense adverse et s'en allait marquer du droit. (ASSE-AFCA : 7-0, 72ème).Joris Rochette faisait un festival côté droit et son centre en retrait trouvait Riad Moutae qui  parachevait cette belle action. (ASSE-AFCA : 8-0, 76ème).Enfin, alors qu'il restait une dizaine de minutes à jouer, une belle passe cachée de Lenny Nebout pour Hugo Durin permettait à ce dernier de conclure d'un tir entre le gardien et son poteau d'une frappe décroisée. C'était son 6ème but de l'après-midi ! (ASSE-AFCA : 9-0, 81ème). Le match à sens unique se terminait sur ce score fleuve qui reflétait bien la sérieuse prestation des jeunes Verts.

La composition : Clapeyron - R.Fontaine, Ahamadi, Balta, Moser - Evaristo, Rochette, Moussaoui (Boukerma, 60ème)- Nebout, Durin, N.Fontaine (Moutae, 60ème).

La réaction de Kevin de Jesus : "Satisfait du match ? Certes, mais mon but c'est de voir des comportements qui progressent, qui avancent vers ce que j'exige des jeunes. A la mi-temps, le résultat semblait acquis, mais j'ai demandé à mes joueurs de continuer à trouver des solutions pour déséquilibrer l'équipe adverse, de varier les combinaisons pour arriver à cet objectif, en diversifiant les circuits, en impliquant toute l'équipe dans cette recherche, défense y compris, par les montées des excentrés dans leur couloir, des centraux dans l'axe pour tenter des frappes de loin. J'ai apprécié le sérieux des joueurs qui sont restés sérieux, sans s'enflammer, en respectant l'adversaire. Cet esprit-là est important."

"Mario Is My Life" - Beloved Blogger 'Supper Mario Broth' Reveals Health Issues

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

"Only blindness or death will result in me no longer posting Mario facts"

The author of the beloved Mario-related blog Supper Mario Broth has posted a health update revealing he has heart failure.

This comes via a social post in which he states he has "a 2/3 chance of surviving past one year with this condition." Despite "a lifelong commitment to a healthy lifestyle", he says his decline in health is due to underlying genetic causes.

A complication from his diagnosis is retinopathy, which is essentially the degradation of the retina that can result in a loss of sight and may affect his ability to research Mario games, post facts, and run the blog in the future.

I am scared I will soon not be able to see Mario again. However, I promise that until that day arrives, I will always be here, posting Mario facts. Thank you all so much for your support through the years.

Despite this news, he has stressed an ongoing commitment to Supper Mario Broth, stating that "only blindness or death will result in me no longer posting Mario facts." He goes on to say that the announcement is not a request for help before thanking readers for all their support over the years.

His posts have been inundated with support. User 'rainbow batsuit' says, "Even if you never post a single fact again, the legacy of joy and discovery you have already built will live on in our hearts for untold years." All of us here at Nintendo Life wholeheartedly agree.

The blog's latest post relating to Mario reveals that it's actually possible to use Dry Bones in Super Mario Maker 2 to play the ocarina in Zelda: Ocarina of Time on Switch 2 via the console's built-in mic.

In The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for Nintendo Switch 2, it is possible to play the ocarina using Dry Bones in Super Mario Maker 2.

We're sure the entire Nintendo Life community will join us in sending Broth our thanks and very best wishes.

Military radar can make your router switch Wi-Fi channels - here's why

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

You might notice that every so often, your dual-band router drops its 5 GHz network without warning. Phones and laptops connected to it drop down to the slower 2.4 GHz band, video calls begin to stutter and freeze, and a few minutes later, everything comes back on a different channel. If you take a look in your router's event log afterward, you might notice the word "RADAR" appearing throughout.

That's Dynamic Frequency Selection, or DFS, in action. According to ASUS, it's how the router gives way to military radar, satellite communication and weather radar that has priority on large parts of the 5 GHz band. In the United States, Federal Communications Commission (FCC) rules require a router to listen for radar on one of those shared channels for 60 seconds before it transmits, to stop within 10 seconds of hearing one and to stay off that channel for at least 30 minutes afterward. DFS is switched on by default, and the FCC requires it to stay that way in the shared bands.

The radar that's most likely to interfere with a US home network isn't the military, though, it's those found at airports. The Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) runs 45 Terminal Doppler Weather Radar systems that protect 46 of the country's busiest airports, from JFK and O'Hare to Dallas and Denver. Their job is to spot wind shear, the sudden and potentially dangerous change in wind speed and direction that has historically caused aviation accidents during approach and departure; they transmit between 5600 and 5650 MHz right in the middle of the 5 GHz Wi-Fi band. Per the FAA, no wind shear accident has occurred at a TDWR-protected airport since the system was commissioned in 1994.

In early 2009, the FAA began detecting interference to those radars caused by unlicensed 5 GHz equipment, prompting a joint investigation with the National Telecommunications and Information Administration (NTIA). Enforcement followed, and in August 2019, the FCC proposed $25,000 fines against three wireless ISPs — Boom Solutions, Integra Wireless and WinPR — after their equipment interfered with the weather radar at the San Juan International Airport in Puerto Rico. In each case, the FCC said the companies had turned DFS off. WinPR's penalty was confirmed that November and Boom Solutions' in early 2020. While those were outdoor point-to-point links and not home routers, they're a good example of why router manufacturers can't simply drop a feature because it annoys their customers.

That government-mandated 60-second listen happens each time a router spins up on a DFS channel, which is why 5 GHz can take longer than 2.4 GHz to reappear after you restart your router. ASUS says the wait can take anywhere from one to 10 minutes depending on the country. The upper end of the scale is in Europe, where the ETSI standard governing 5 GHz Wi-Fi demands a 10-minute check on any channel that overlaps the 5600 to 5650 MHz weather radar slot.

Radar detected mid-session triggers the 10-second exit and 30-minute lockout. FRITZ! says its routers steer connected devices to 2.4 GHz while they wait, "seamlessly" for devices that support the 802.11k and 802.11v roaming standards and by briefly dropping the connection for older ones. ASUS, meanwhile, notes that a radar hit on a 160 MHz channel can push the router down to 80 MHz, and that a router set to a fixed channel won't move back once the radar goes away.

So, it's fine to use DFS channels. They're legal, enabled out of the box and most of the 5 GHz band is made up of them; only channels 36 to 48 and 149 to 165 are radar-free in the United States. The trade-off is occasional interruptions if you live near somewhere like an airport or military base. FRITZ!'s threshold is radar detected continuously or every hour, at which point it recommends pinning the router to channels 36 to 48.

ASUS provides instructions for disabling automatic DFS channels that also tell users to untick 160 MHz channel bandwidth, because the widest 5 GHz channels can't be built without the radar-shared spectrum.

So, by turning DFS off, you'll get some extra stability at the cost of losing out on the fastest 5 GHz connection your router can offer. That's worth thinking about before you go hunting for tweaks that could speed up your router. You'll find these settings alongside the options you'd change to improve your router's security, usually under the 5 GHz wireless menu.

Blackburn Rovers, Birmingham City track Chelsea starlet after Tony Mowbray snubbed summer loan deal

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City have both performed positively over the first quarter of the 2026/27 campaign, but they’ll have eyes on the January transfer window as an opportunity to strengthen the ranks further. Now, TEAMtalk has claimed one player of interest to both clubs is Kendry Paez.

The report states that Chelsea are actively looking for potential loan destinations for Ecuadorian prospect Paez. Rovers and the Blues are keen, but the 19-year-old also has admirers at Celtic and Trabzonspor, and he was eyed by Belgium’s KV Mechelen in the summer.

Paez was on the radar at Ewood Park in the last transfer window as was put to Tony Mowbray as a potential signing. However, the boss snubbed the deal, and the attacking midfielder has remained with Chelsea instead.

Paez was viewed as one of South America’s most exciting talents when he officially joined Chelsea in 2025. However, loans at RC Strasbourg and River Plate have not yielded the success hoped.

Blackburn Rovers were lined up as a potential destination in Paez’s bid to settle into English football, but after Mowbray opted against bringing him in, he has only played U21s football.

Paez has made six appearances across the Premier League 2 and EFL Trophy. He provided an assist in a 2-1 defeat to Wycombe Wanderers in September and played a little over an hour in a 2-0 triumph over Reading in the same cup competitions in October.

At 19, the Chelsea starlet is still young and has plenty of time to come good in England. But, Paez has struggled to impact games the way he did in his emergence in Ecuador with Independiente del Valle, so it remains to be seen if a half-season loan to the Championship can breathe life into his career again.

This applies to Birmingham City more given that they are the current Championship leaders, but clubs have to be careful with loan deals in the winter. Recruitment staff and managers alike need to be wary of how new signings will impact a thriving squad.

Loan players come in with a desire to play, but it can be tough to find opportunities in a thriving team. If that were to happen to Paez, it wouldn’t help his development and could bring players off the pitch too.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t in quite the same position as they’re 13th, but Mowbray has harnessed some strong squad spirit. The fact the boss has already snubbed the loan signing of Paez would raise questions over a winter deal for his services too.

Time will tell what comes of Paez’s winter transfer fate as Chelsea look for potential destinations.

Apple Intelligence registered in China for use on iPads and Macs

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Shanghai’s cyberspace regulator approved the filing weeks before Apple’s AI service, powered by Alibaba’s Qwen, launches in China.

The Apple Store in Nanjing, China, lit up at night.

Apple’s AI service, Apple Intelligence, has been cleared for use on tablets and laptops in China. The cyberspace regulator in Shanghai made the change on Monday, Reuters reported.

The regulator said Apple’s iPads and Mac laptops had been added as two new uses in the service’s filing. It made the change under China’s rules for AI services.

In China, firms must register AI models and chatbot-style services with the state before they can offer them to the public.

Apple Intelligence first won approval for use on iPhones in China in mid-July. At the time, Alibaba said its Qwen AI model would power the service on Apple’s devices in China. Baidu is also a tech partner, Bloomberg reported.

“Qwen will be integrated into Apple Intelligence experiences within iOS, iPadOS, macOS, and visionOS for users in China,” an Alibaba spokesperson told CNBC in July.

Apple first showed Apple Intelligence in 2024 and began rolling it out in other markets later that year, with iOS 18. China has waited much longer.

Apple needs local partners in China. OpenAI’s ChatGPT, which runs some Apple Intelligence tools in other countries, is not offered there.

On 31 October, Apple said on its China news site that the first features would come with iOS 27.3. A test version for app makers went live on 4 November. The full release is due in December.

In China, Qwen will run Siri, Writing Tools, and visual intelligence, which lets users ask about what the camera sees. But Siri AI and other tools Apple showed off at its June developer event are not part of the launch.

Apple’s October news listed only iPhones. These are the iPhone 15 Pro and 15 Pro Max, the iPhone Air, the iPhone Duo, and every model from the iPhone 16 on. Siri and the phone must be set to English or Simplified Chinese.

Reuters also reported in August that Apple had built its own AI model just for China with Alibaba’s help. That was a shift from its plan to rely only on outside models in the country.

Apple did not say on Monday when the features will reach iPads and Macs in China.

Researchers warn that warmer weather is helping mosquitoes spread dangerous diseases like malaria and dengue

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A century of data shows bed nets, drugs and growing cities explain much of why malaria and dengue went separate ways as the planet warmed.

Scientists have long predicted that a warmer world would bring more disease from mosquitoes, and dengue has kept rising along with the heat. Malaria went the other way.

Over a century of increasing temperatures, the area where malaria was a constant presence shrank to about half of what it had been, while dengue infections climbed to record highs.

According to a new review of the evidence, much of that split comes from outside the weather. People pushed malaria back with decades of bed nets, spraying, and drugs, while the growth of cities favored the mosquitoes that carry dengue.

Toby Cumming, a research fellow at the Health and Climate Initiative in Monash University’s School of Public Health, led the work with three colleagues. It is a review of studies that other teams have already published, not a new experiment.

In an interview with Earth.com, Cumming said the climate in many parts of the world is becoming more suitable for diseases spread by mosquitoes. “We cannot draw a straight line from climate suitability to rates of malaria and dengue transmission,” Cumming added.

Both diseases reach people through a bite. Malaria comes from a parasite carried by Anopheles mosquitoes, which bite mostly at night. Dengue comes from a virus carried by Aedes mosquitoes, which bite during the day.

Mosquitoes are cold-blooded, so their body temperature follows the air around them. At 59°F (15°C), a young Aedes aegypti takes 40 days to become an adult. At 95°F (35°C), it takes seven.

Warmth also makes mosquitoes bite more often. It also shortens the time the parasite or virus needs to develop inside the insect before it can infect a person.

There’s a ceiling, though. Past a certain point, heat starts to hurt the mosquito. Models suggest malaria spreads best at about 77°F to 82°F (25°C to 28°C). For dengue, it’s about 79°F to 84°F (26°C to 29°C).

Rain works in both directions. Heavy downpours can wash eggs and larvae away, while drought can leave stagnant pools in riverbeds and push families to store water in open containers near their homes.

Asked how people can protect themselves from dengue, Cumming said the best thing is to avoid bites, with long, loose clothing and insect repellent. Emptying containers that hold water around the home helps too.

The authors put numbers on how much warming has already shifted the odds. Between the 1950s and the decade ending in 2024, the climate’s estimated potential to spread dengue rose 12 percent for Aedes aegypti and 49 percent for its cousin, Aedes albopictus.

Over the same stretch, the area where the climate suits malaria grew by only about 2 percent. In Africa, the malaria map is moving.

One analysis cited in the review found that the elevation limit of malaria mosquitoes rose about 21 feet (6.5 meters) a year from 1898 to 2016.

Even so, the authors call the evidence for climate-driven shifts in malaria more contested than for dengue.

Dengue’s spread is easier to see. The disease has spread locally in places where it hadn’t before, including the southern United States, and it’s showing up more often in mainland France. Earth.com has also reported on rising dengue risk in California.

More than 14 million dengue cases were reported to the World Health Organization in 2024. That was more than double the previous annual record.

The longest view comes from a 2010 analysis in the journal Nature. Around 1900, malaria was a constant presence across an estimated 58 percent of the world’s land. By 2007, that figure had fallen to 30 percent.

Within the smaller area, far fewer people carried the parasite. In Africa, the share of people infected with the deadliest malaria parasite fell from 40 percent in 1900 to 1929, to 24 percent in 2010 to 2015.

Europe shows the same pattern. Malaria began to fade there in the 1800s while temperatures were rising, and the review credits better living conditions, better health care, and improved irrigation and drainage.

Cold didn’t stop the disease, either. Malaria was common in the marshy parishes of England from the 16th to the 18th centuries, during an especially cold period. In Finland, mosquitoes survived freezing winters by sheltering inside houses and stables.

The two diseases differ first in the tools people built to fight them. Efforts to wipe out malaria go back to the 1950s, with house spraying and mass drug campaigns, and later with bed nets treated with insecticide.

Global spending on malaria rose from $1.5 billion to $4.3 billion between 2000 and 2016. Over that time, the yearly rate of new cases fell 36 percent and the death rate fell 60 percent.

“What started as a climate story – the impact of temperature, rainfall and humidity on malaria and dengue – became a human story,” Cumming told Earth.com.

Dengue had no matching toolkit. There are still no drugs that treat the virus, vaccines are only now arriving, and bed nets do little against mosquitoes that bite in daylight.

And mosquitoes are building resistance to insecticides, a problem the authors flag for both diseases.

Cities are the second difference. Aedes mosquitoes lay eggs in gutters and water containers, and cities offer plenty of those, along with lots of people to bite.

Anopheles prefer more natural pools, which cities tend to lack, so urban growth tends to cut malaria.

The authors flag one important exception. Anopheles stephensi, a malaria mosquito from Asia that breeds in artificial containers, has now settled into cities in the Horn of Africa.

The good news on malaria has slowed. New cases per 1,000 people at risk fell from 79 in 2000 to 59 in 2015. By 2024, the figure was back up to 64.

In 18 countries, the rate rose by at least 70 percent between 2015 and 2024. Resistance to key malaria drugs is a growing concern too.

Extreme weather can undo progress quickly. After record flooding hit Pakistan in 2022, malaria cases there rose fivefold.

Asked what should be done first to curb the spread, Cumming said the answer is a renewed commitment from the global community to fight malaria and dengue.

That includes scaling up public health measures that work and speeding up research on new ways to prevent and treat both diseases.

The forecasts don’t agree on malaria. Some models predict it will spread into new areas as the planet warms. Others predict that hotter, drier conditions will shrink it in parts of Africa where it’s common today.

Adding wealth to a model changes the picture. Using climate alone, one model estimated that 5.2 billion people would be at risk of malaria by 2050. When it also counted each country’s economic output per person, the estimate dropped to 2.0 billion.

Much of the temperature evidence also comes from lab studies that were never checked in the field. And mosquitoes may adapt to local heat, which fixed temperature rules can’t capture.

For dengue, newer tools are arriving. One vaccine has been licensed in many countries since 2022. The World Mosquito Program, a not-for-profit group owned by Monash University, releases mosquitoes carrying a bacterium called Wolbachia, which makes them less able to spread the virus.

It’s still unclear whether those tools can do for dengue what bed nets and drugs did for malaria. In order to find out, researchers will need years of dengue case records from the places that use them.

The full study was published in the journal Science Advances.
